   @Override\n        public boolean canGoForward() {\n            Constants.AuthMethod authMethod = selectionMapping.get(selection.getSelectedItemPosition());\n\n            if (authMethod == passwordConfirm.getText().toString();\n\n                if (! password.isEmpty()) {\n                    if (password.length() < minLength) {\n                        displayWarning(lengthWarning);\n                        return false;\n                    } else {\n                        if (! confirm.isEmpty() && confirm.equals(password)) {\n                            hideWarning();\n                            return true;\n                        } else {\n                            displayWarning(confirmPasswordWarning);\n                            return false;\n android.os.Build.VERSION_CODES.LOLLIPOP) {\n                    displayWarning(R.string.settings_toast_auth_device_pre_lollipop);\n                    return false;\n                } else if (! km.isKeyguardSecure()) {\n                    displayWarning(R.string.settings_toast_auth_device_not_secure);\n                    return false;\n                }\n\n       selectionMapping.put(i, Constants.EncryptionType.valueOf(encValues[i].toUpperCase()));\n        }\n\n        public Constants.EncryptionType getEncryptionType() {\n            return   }\n\n        public void updateEncryptionType(Constants.EncryptionType newEncryptionType) {\n            if (newEncryptionType == null || newEncryptionType == encryptionType)\n                return;\n\n            encryptionType = newEncryptionType;\n\n            // The views have not been created yet, they will be initialized based on the\n            // new encryption type as soon as desc.setText(R.string.intro_slide3_desc_keystore);\n        }\n\n        /** With password based encryption an authentication is mandatory, with the Android\n         *  KeyStore it is optional and therefore disabled by default. */\n        private Constants.AuthMethod getDefaultAuthMethod() {\n            if (encryptionType == Constants.EncryptionType.PASSWORD)\n                return Constants.AuthMethod.PASSWORD;\n            else\n                return Constants.AuthMethod.NONE;\n        }\n\n        /** Checks if an authentication method is still a sensible choice for the currently\n         *  selected encryption type. */\n        private boolean canKeepAuthMethod(Constants.AuthMethod method) {\n            if (encryptionType == Constants.EncryptionType.PASSWORD) {\n                // Password based encryption needs either a password or a PIN\n                return method == Constants.AuthMethod.PASSWORD || method == Constants.AuthMethod.PIN;\n            } else {\n                // Everything is allowed with the KeyStore, but a password or PIN the user has\n                // not started to fill in yet is only the leftover mandatory default and would\n                // block the navigation, even though the slide says it is optional.\n                if (method == Constants.AuthMethod.PASSWORD || method == Constants.AuthMethod.PIN)\n                    return passwordInput != null && passwordInput.getText() != null &&\n                            passwordInput.getText().length() > 0;\n\n                return true;\n            }\n        }\n\n        private void setAuthMethod(Constants.AuthMethod method) {\n            authMethod = method;\n\n            if (selection != null && selectionMapping != null)\n                selection.setSelection(selectionMapping.indexOfValue(method));\n        }\n\n        private void generateSelectionMapping() {\n            Constants.AuthMethod[] authValues = Constants.AuthMethod.values();\n\n            selectionMapping = new SparseArray<>();\n            for (int i = 0; i < authValues.length; i++)\n                selectionMapping.put(i, authValues[i]);\n        }\n\n        private void displayWarning(int resId) {\n            displayWarning(getString(resId));\n        }\n\n        private void displayWarning(String warning) {\n            authWarnings.setVisibility(View.VISIBLE);\n            authWarnings.setText(warning);\n        }\n\n        private void hideWarning() {\n            authWarnings.setVisibility(View.GONE);\n        }\n\n        public void flashWarning() {\n            if (authWarnings.getVisibility() == View.VISIBLE) {\n                ObjectAnimator animator = ObjectAnimator.ofInt(authWarnings, "backgroundColor",\n                        Color.TRANSPARENT, getResources().getColor(R.color.colorAccent), Color.TRANSPARENT);\n                animator.setDuration(500);\n                animator.setRepeatCount(0);\n                animator.setInterpolator(new AccelerateDecelerateInterpolator());\n                animator.setEvaluator(new ArgbEvaluator());\n Here\'s what was wrong and what I changed.\n\n## Root cause\n\nBoth symptoms lived in `app/src/main/java/.../Activities/IntroScreenActivity.java`.\n\n**1. Slide 3 showed the wrong encryption method.** `EncryptionFragment.onCreateView()` ended with `setSelection(...PASSWORD)`, whose listener fires the `onEncryptionChanged` callback into `AuthenticationFragment.updateEncryptionType()`. But slide 3\'s views don\'t exist that early, and the old method was wrapped in `if (desc != null) { ... }` — so the update was silently dropped. `AuthenticationFragment.onCreateView()` never applied the stored `encryptionType` afterwards, so the layout\'s hardcoded `android:text="@string/intro_slide3_desc_keystore"` stayed on screen. That\'s also why the spinner *was* correctly greying out "None"/"Device credentials" (that reads the `encryptionType` field, which was right) while the text disagreed — and why re-selecting the dropdown "fixed" it: only then did the callback arrive with views in place.\n\n**2. Contradictory red warning under KeyStore.** The auth method was hardcoded to `PASSWORD` (`selection.setSelection(...AuthMethod.PASSWORD)`) regardless of encryption type, and switching to KeyStore never revised it — leaving a navigation-blocking "Please set a password to continue!" next to text saying it was optional.\n\n## Fix\n\n- **Single source of truth for the encryption type:** `EncryptionFragment` now keeps the selection in a field (default `PASSWORD`, as before) instead of reading it back off the spinner, and `updateDescription()` is called explicitly in `onCreateView()` since `setSelection()` doesn\'t fire the listener when the position is unchanged. This also makes the choice survive ViewPager view re-creation rather than snapping back to `PASSWORD`.\n- **Slide 3 initialises from the stored type:** `onCreateView()` now calls `updateDescription()` and derives the initial auth method, so a change that arrived before the views existed is applied when they are created. The activity also seeds `authenticationFragment.updateEncryptionType(encryptionFragment.getEncryptionType())` in `onCreate()`, making the result independent of fragment creation order.\n- **Encryption-dependent default auth method** (`getDefaultAuthMethod()`): `PASSWORD` for password-based encryption (mandatory), `NONE` for KeyStore (optional) — the behaviour the reporter suggested.\n- **`canKeepAuthMethod()`** decides on encryption change whether to keep the current choice: under password encryption it forces `PASSWORD`/`PIN` (prior behaviour preserved); under KeyStore it drops a leftover `PASSWORD`/`PIN` default back to `NONE` *only when the password field is still empty*, so the blocking warning disappears without discarding credentials a user already typed. `updateEncryptionType()` also early-returns when the type is unchanged, so re-picking the same dropdown entry can\'t wipe a deliberate auth choice, and it calls `notifyDataSetChanged()` so the greyed-out entries refresh.\n\nTwo small robustness items came along with it, since the new code can now touch slide 3 while it\'s detached: `getPassword()` and `canGoForward()` no longer dereference possibly-null views, and the spinner listener bails out when `!isAdded()` before calling `getString()`/`getIntroActivity()`/`updateNavigation()` (the latter throws `IllegalStateException` when unattached).\n\nVerified with `./gradlew clean assembleDebug` — **BUILD SUCCESSFUL**.
